## Finalized Report / Current Recommendation

`ProtocolSend` is not supported as an original standalone compilation unit, class, header, global sender interface, or aggregate emitter. The strongest reconstruction is a reviewed negative source-unit page at [UID:0000MV] that records why no `ProtocolSend.cpp` or `ProtocolSend.h` should be generated. The central outbound queue corridor at `0x00574ae0-0x00575377` is a contiguous family of `Socket` members compiled with `Socket.cpp`; the queue primitive at `0x00596960` remains inherited `Thread` infrastructure; `PacketBuffer` starts independently at `0x00575380`; packet-transform consumers remain in `PacketTransform`; and the 416 direct calls to `Socket::QueueAndSendPacket` originate in feature modules whose packet builders remain feature-local.

The target retains `PROPOSED_RECONSTRUCTION_PATH:"NONE"`, `CANONICAL_OWNER:NONE`, blank formal CPP/H, and non-emitting treatment. The accepted callback applied the assignment-time `87/88 -> 96/97` recommendation, so the current post-callback score is `96/97`. That score is justified because this pass exhaustively tested the proposed source unit, candidate function corridor, ownership alternatives, raw starts, globals, generated output, caller fanout, queue/transport boundaries, packet builders, and negative symbol/pointer evidence. It remains below `100/100` because stripped binary evidence cannot recover the literal original source filename set or exclude an unused source-only header with mathematical certainty; that archival uncertainty does not justify emitting a speculative file.

The same evidence exposed support-document defects at the accepted report-only Gate 1 artifact: missing source bodies/declarations for exact Socket helpers, stale comment-only treatment of two source-shaped raw helpers, a stale filename/title and Application accessor names for [UID:0001HW], stale `CashShopRequest` labels, stale caller counts, and incomplete Socket header/source output. The authorized ordinary callbacks closed the Socket, Config, StringBase, Application, MemoryMan, global, and PacketTransform declaration portions at source level. The narrow callback corrected `by-item/PacketTransformHelpers.md` and validator-generated `PacketTransform.h` from stale `int BuildHandshakeBlock(size_t size, void *block);` to source-closed `void BuildHandshakeBlock(size_t seedLength, void *seedBlock);`, preserving the ABI established by UID0003SP and the accepted definition. The 2026-08-18 Gate 2A repair callback then removed stale provisional-owner, obsolete score, and removed-`simroot_v2` authority text from the thirteen exact ordinary destinations recorded below. No repair is routed through UID0000MV formal source.

## Inference Research Guidance Check

- Runtime behavior controls all reconstruction choices.
- Source-facing names are inferred as realistic human names, never preserved as `sub_...`, `dword_...`, `byte_...`, or decompiler temporaries.
- Consistency with established `Socket`, `Thread`, `PacketBuffer`, and `Application` naming is used after behavior and source shape, not ahead of them.
- Address adjacency alone was not used to prove ownership. Ownership is supported by receiver provenance, class lifetime writes, member offsets, dispatcher consumption, source-order boundaries, and absence of a competing object/type/global.
- Negative evidence was used to reject a standalone ProtocolSend file, not to suppress exact source-shaped Socket methods.
- Missing original names are resolved with best-supported human spellings and explicit confidence rather than deferred as future investigation.
- No Wave2/Wave3 assertion is used as current proof.

## Heuristic / Inference Reanalysis And Validation

The central heuristic question was whether a broad API used by many feature modules implies a dedicated protocol-send source unit. It does not. All representative call sites load `g_packetSender` into `ECX` immediately before calling `0x00574bb0`; the storage is typed `Socket *`; five writes are confined to Socket construction/destruction/unwind; and the called function reads a Socket field at `+0x3aa4e` before posting command 8 to its inherited Thread queue. This is an ordinary widely used member of a central transport object, not evidence for a separate request class.

The nearby source order independently supports the same conclusion. Socket construction/destruction ends at `0x00574ad8`, eight `0xcc` bytes lead to command wrappers at `0x00574ae0`, the queue wrappers and status packet methods continue through `0x00575377`, nine `0xcc` bytes follow, and the separate PacketBuffer helper family begins at `0x00575380`. Every internal gap is alignment. The dispatcher at `0x00575e60` consumes command IDs 3-14 as Socket operations. A separate ProtocolSend compilation unit would require an unexplained mid-class split while retaining the same `this`, private fields, Thread base methods, and Socket dispatcher.

The two raw starts at `0x00574b70` and `0x00574d00` remain coherent source-shaped member bodies despite zero external start xrefs and no VA/RVA pointer hits. Their intact prologue/epilogue, exact argument cleanup, placement in the Socket method island, and command cases 13/14 establish source semantics. The correct preservation choice is to reconstruct them as retained Socket methods and preserve their currently unreferenced status in prose, not to replace source with comments.

## Evidence Standards Used

- Direct: current MCP function/item boundaries, bytes, decompilation/disassembly, xrefs, stack frames, names/types/comments, globals, and dispatcher behavior.
- Corroborating: current by-* metadata/formal channels, dated validator-generated source snapshots, caller-side docs, and accepted project naming/type decisions.
- Negative: no ProtocolSend string/entity, no dedicated generated file, no independent sender type/RTTI/vtable/global, no raw-start pointer hits, and no competing lifetime owner.
- Inferred: original source filenames, exact private method spellings, and field/accessor spellings where symbols are stripped.
- Rejected as authority: stale generated aliases, any disassembly-rendered `unk_...` spelling that is not also returned literally by current public `inspect_items.name`, stale CashShop ownership, Wave2/Wave3 text, and old caller-count summaries. The current P06 `unk_...` names recorded below are accepted prestates only because the attested current-hash read returned those exact public names.

Claims that affect source output are stated at report-level detail and tied to exact destinations in the ledger. No implementation outcome is used to excuse missing research.

## Positive Evidence Summary

- `g_packetSender` is currently typed `Socket *` at `0x0067a7ec`; its five direct writes are Socket construction/destruction/unwind/scalar-destruction paths.
- Representative queue calls set `ECX` from `g_packetSender`, proving the receiver rather than merely sharing a helper address.
- `Socket::QueueAndSendPacket` reads Socket field `+0x3aa4e`, owns the copy/terminator policy, and posts command 8 to the inherited queue.
- `Socket::HandleSocketCommand` consumes commands 3-14 generated by the adjacent wrappers.
- Source order is continuous from Socket lifecycle through request wrappers/status packets and stops at the PacketBuffer start.
- Current by-* ownership and the dated generated snapshots route the corridor through `network/Socket.cpp` and `Socket.h`.
- Feature packet builders across chat, user, map, ranking, mail, board, clan and other modules call the Socket member but retain their own packet/opcode logic.

## Ranked Ownership Analysis

1. **Socket.cpp / Socket class: accepted.** Same receiver, same private fields, same Thread base queue, same dispatcher, Socket-only global lifetime, contiguous source order, and validator-routed generated root; physical generated state remains subject to dynamic Gate 2A reread.
2. **Feature-local packet builders: accepted for callers only.** They construct opcodes/payloads and call the Socket API; they do not own the central method.
3. **Thread.cpp: dependency only.** It owns queue records, semaphore signaling and sync result handoff, but not Socket command meanings or packet-copy policy.
4. **PacketBuffer.cpp: dependency only.** It owns scalar readers/writers beginning at `0x00575380`; it does not own the Socket object or queue.
5. **PacketTransform.cpp: dependency/consumer support only.** It owns transform state and command-14 processing, not the posting wrapper.
6. **CashShopRequest.cpp/class: rejected.** Separate global/object, no lifetime relationship, no receiver provenance, and feature-specific downloader semantics.
7. **ProtocolSend.cpp/header/class: rejected.** No independent symbol/type/object/range/source order/generated output and all candidate code already has stronger ownership.

## Source Placement

Accepted central placement remains:

- `NexusTK/network/Socket.cpp`: all 18 source-shaped corridor members in binary order through existing child emission.
- `NexusTK/network/Socket.h`: complete Socket declarations, including currently omitted command 7, 9, 13 and 14 methods.
- `NexusTK/util/Thread.cpp/.h`: inherited queue/event implementation.
- `NexusTK/network/PacketBuffer.cpp/.h`: scalar serializers/readers from `0x00575380`.
- `NexusTK/network/PacketTransform.cpp/.h`: transform/handshake support.

## Range / Split / Padding / Reclassification Analysis

The candidate central range is bounded by Socket destructor tail padding before `0x00574ae0` and PacketBuffer padding before `0x00575380`. Its 18 function-shaped units are separated only by conventional alignment. No internal boundary coincides with a new object lifetime, vtable, global receiver, source string, or independent helper family. The historical aggregate starts at `0x00574b90`, which cuts off six earlier wrappers that use the same `this` and dispatcher; this proves it is a documentation slice, not a compilation-unit boundary.

The raw command-13 body ends at `0x00574b83` and is followed by 13 `0xcc` bytes. The raw command-14 body ends at `0x00574d3f` and is followed by one `0xcc` byte. Both are source-shaped retained methods, not padding or compiler thunks. The successor `0x00575370` getter ends at `0x00575377`; nine `0xcc` bytes cleanly separate PacketBuffer at `0x00575380`.

## IDA Rename / Type / Comment Recommendations

Supervisor Gate 2B only. This report performs zero IDA mutations. Bind exact normalized `E:\NTK\Resources\NexusTK\NexusTK.exe.i64` as `CANONICAL`; it is read-only IDA evidence and the backup source and is never an IDA/MCP mutation or save target. Before any MCP open, record a separate immutable physical `CANONICAL` path/size/SHA256 receipt; create, durably flush, close, and verify a distinct collision-safe prechange backup path bound as `BACKUP`; create a distinct isolated transaction copy bound as `TX`; and record separate physical receipts for all three. Hard-stop unless `BACKUP` and `TX` are byte-identical to `CANONICAL`, all paths are normalized and pairwise distinct, and the sealed `BACKUP` remains immutable through final closure. The first IDA request is literally `idb_open {"input_path":"TX","mode":"force_headless","build_caches":true,"init_hexrays":true,"run_auto_analysis":false,"idle_ttl_sec":7200}`; `preferred_session_id` is omitted so no report-invented session identity can be reused. Success requires `success:true`, no `error`, a present `session`, exact `session.input_path == TX`, a nonempty returned `session.session_id`, `session.is_analyzing:false`, and no cache or Hex-Rays warmup failure. Bind report identifier `DB` only to that exact returned `session.session_id`. The primary request is `runtime_attestation({expected_database:DB,expected_canonical_path:TX})`; its runtime receipt records DB/TX identity and proves `CANONICAL` was never opened by the mutation session, while distinct filesystem receipts record `CANONICAL`/`BACKUP`/`TX` physical identity. Neither proof substitutes for the other. An existing session identifier, `idb_list` observation, attestation response, preferred name, canonical path, or literal text `DB`/`TX` never creates these bindings. Every endpoint name, request key, address, range, spelling, type, comment, frame invariant, readback, and hard stop below is literal. Every `set_type` parser-input signature is a complete named declaration with named receiver/parameters. The public persistent endpoints are `define_func`, `rename`, `set_type`, and `set_repeatable_comments`; `force_recompile` is nonpersistent; readback uses only the named public endpoints below.

### AT01 Public Runtime Attestation And Boundary Contract

AT01 is mandatory fail-closed protection for every Section 21 action and protection row; a basic DB/TX `ok:true` receipt is insufficient. Each transaction-role AT01 call is literally `runtime_attestation({expected_database:DB,expected_canonical_path:TX})`, with `DB` and `TX` replaced only by that role's exact freshly returned session and normalized opened path; later named roles use the literal calls specified below. The following SHA256 values are compact identities of the exact expected byte strings freshly reread through those literal requests. They do not replace byte comparison: after each A row, reread that row's exact A range with the same one-region schema and require its returned `data` string to be byte-for-byte identical to the retained prestate; after A18, repeat all three batch requests and require every final `data` string byte-for-byte identical to its retained prestate as well as matching the size and SHA256 below. Formal Socket CPP include block and complete corridor bodies:

```cpp
#include "Socket.h"
#include "PacketTransform.h"
#include "../app/Application.h"
#include "../app/StartupWindow.h"
#include "../config/Config.h"
#include "../util/MemoryMan.h"
#include "../util/StringBase.h"

#include <set>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

namespace
{
typedef mystr::StringBase<wchar_t> WideFriendName;
typedef mystr::StringBase<char> NarrowFriendName;

struct WideFriendNameLess
{
    bool operator()(const WideFriendName& left,
                    const WideFriendName& right) const
    {
        return mystr::WideRangeCompare(
            left.c_str(), left.c_str() + left.length(),
            right.c_str(), right.c_str() + right.length()) < 0;
    }
};

typedef std::set<WideFriendName, WideFriendNameLess> FriendNameSet;
}

void Socket::PostConnectWithFallbackCommand(char transportMode,
                                             void *connectionParameter)
{
    DispatchRequest(4,
                    static_cast<int>(static_cast<signed char>(transportMode)),
                    reinterpret_cast<int>(connectionParameter));
}

void Socket::PostConnectCommand(char transportMode,
                                void *connectionParameter)
{
    DispatchRequest(3,
                    static_cast<int>(static_cast<signed char>(transportMode)),
                    reinterpret_cast<int>(connectionParameter));
}

void Socket::PostResetConnectionCommand()
{
    DispatchRequest(5, 0, 0);
}

HANDLE Socket::PostConnectionStatusQuery(bool *outConnected)
{
    return DispatchRequestAndReturnEvent(
        12, reinterpret_cast<int>(outConnected), 1, 0, 0);
}

void Socket::PostReconnectEndpointCommand(unsigned long hostAddress,
                                           unsigned short port)
{
    DispatchRequest(6, static_cast<int>(hostAddress), port);
}

void Socket::PostPacketTransformTableCommand(unsigned char tableMode)
{
    DispatchRequest(13, tableMode, 0);
}

void Socket::PostSocketReceiveCommand(void *receiveBuffer, int receiveSize)
{
    DispatchRequest(7, reinterpret_cast<int>(receiveBuffer), receiveSize);
}

void Socket::QueueAndSendPacket(const void *packetData, short packetSize)
{
    if (m_sendDisabled)
        return;

    const int copySize = static_cast<int>(packetSize);
    MemoryMan *memoryMan = GetMemoryMan();
    char *queuedPacket = static_cast<char *>(
        memoryMan->AllocateBufferMemory(copySize + 1));
    memoryMan->MemmoveWrapper(queuedPacket, packetData, copySize);
    queuedPacket[copySize] = '\0';
    DispatchRequest(8, reinterpret_cast<int>(queuedPacket), copySize + 1);
}

void Socket::PostSocketModeByteCommand(unsigned char mode)
{
    DispatchRequest(9, mode, 0);
}

void Socket::QueueTransportStringCommand(const char *text)
{
    const size_t length = strlen(text);
    MemoryMan *memoryMan = GetMemoryMan();
    char *queuedText = static_cast<char *>(
        memoryMan->AllocateBufferMemory(length + 1));
    memoryMan->MemmoveWrapper(queuedText, text, length);
    queuedText[length] = '\0';
    DispatchRequest(10, reinterpret_cast<int>(queuedText), 0);
}

void Socket::SendSingleByteCommand(unsigned char value)
{
    DispatchRequest(11, value, 0);
}

bool Socket::IsSocketConnectedState() const
{
    return m_transportMode == 5;
}

void Socket::SetSocketSendDisabled(unsigned char disabled)
{
    m_sendDisabled = disabled;
}

void Socket::SetSocketConnectionFlag(unsigned char value)
{
    m_connectionFlag = value;
}

void Socket::QueueHandshakeBlock(unsigned int blockSize,
                                 const void *blockData)
{
    MemoryMan *memoryMan = GetMemoryMan();
    void *queuedBlock = memoryMan->AllocateBufferMemory(blockSize);
    memoryMan->MemmoveWrapper(queuedBlock, blockData, blockSize);
    DispatchRequest(14, blockSize, reinterpret_cast<int>(queuedBlock));
}

void Socket::QueueClientVersionStatusPacket()
{
    unsigned char packet[7];
    unsigned short versionMajor = g_pApplication->GetVersionMajor();
    const unsigned short versionMinor = g_pApplication->GetVersionMinor();

    g_packetSequenceByte = 0;
    if (g_pConfig->m_clientVersionJitterEnabled && rand() < 0x1fff)
        --versionMajor;

    packet[0] = 0;
    packet[1] = static_cast<unsigned char>(versionMajor >> 8);
    packet[2] = static_cast<unsigned char>(versionMajor);
    packet[3] = 0xc5;
    packet[4] = static_cast<unsigned char>(versionMinor >> 8);
    packet[5] = static_cast<unsigned char>(versionMinor);
    packet[6] = static_cast<unsigned char>(g_useEpfAssets);
    QueueAndSendPacket(packet, 7);
}

void Socket::BuildAndSendFriendNameListSync()
{
    unsigned char packet[1024];
    FriendNameSet countedNames;
    FriendNameSet emittedNames;

    packet[0] = 0x77;
    for (int index = 0; index < 20; ++index) {
        const WideFriendName& friendName = g_pConfig->m_friendNames[index];
        if (!friendName.empty())
            countedNames.insert(friendName);
    }

    packet[1] = static_cast<unsigned char>(countedNames.size());
    int packetSize = 2;
    packet[2] = 0;

    for (int index = 0; index < 20; ++index) {
        const WideFriendName& friendName = g_pConfig->m_friendNames[index];
        if (friendName.empty() || !emittedNames.insert(friendName).second)
            continue;

        const NarrowFriendName narrowName(friendName);
        packet[packetSize++] = 0x0c;
        GetMemoryMan()->MemmoveWrapper(
            packet + packetSize, narrowName.c_str(), 12);
        packetSize += 12;
    }

    QueueAndSendPacket(packet, static_cast<short>(packetSize));
}

unsigned char Socket::GetConnectionStatus() const
{
    return m_connectionStatus;
}
```

The friend-list capacity and count are binary-derived, not heuristic. The frame reserves 1,024 packet bytes beginning at `Src`. The body first writes `0x0077`, then overwrites byte 1 with the first-pass unique count. Records begin at offset 2 and are exactly marker `0x0c` plus 12 narrow bytes. Both passes traverse the same twenty `Config::m_friendNames` slots with the same `WideRangeCompare` comparator; the second set is a seen-set during original-slot traversal, so output remains first-occurrence order. The maximum is exactly `2 + 20 * 13 = 262` bytes. `packet[2] = 0` is scratch overwritten by the first record or excluded from the two-byte empty payload. The converting `StringBase` constructor and exact 12-byte copy replace all prior placeholder helpers.

## Final Recommendation

Accept the negative source-unit decision and preserve the applied Socket and PacketTransform support repairs after supervisor Gate 2A verification. UID0000MV is now a high-completion page that prevents future duplicate `ProtocolSend` research rather than an unresolved placeholder. The only generated central sender interface remains `Socket`; packets remain built by their feature owners; `Thread` remains the asynchronous queue mechanism; `PacketBuffer` remains the serializer; `PacketTransform` remains the encoding/handshake dependency.

For Gate 2B, accept the supervisor's unsaved VerifyOnly evidence only as a correction to deterministic public-surface postconditions: A08 and A17 use persisted function-type authority distinct from undeletable/unretypable physical frame artifacts, A15 uses the observed shifted final frame, A06/A15 retain expected bounded Hex-Rays failures, public `callees` controls direct-callee sets, and `xref_query.total` controls incoming totals. These reconciliations do not alter the exact 55 persistent/35 nonpersistent topology and do not establish disk persistence because no save occurred.

## Open Questions With Attempted Resolution

1. **Could an original unused `ProtocolSend.h` have existed?** No binary technique can disprove an unused source-only file absolutely. Project/source topology, lack of dependent types, and complete ownership of all emitted code make it non-reconstructable and non-emitting. Resolved for current source reconstruction: do not create it.
2. **Were the two zero-xref raw helpers handwritten?** Their exact prologue/epilogue, argument cleanup, dispatcher calls, semantic command consumers, and placement between modeled Socket methods are stronger than absent current xrefs. Resolved: retain as Socket source methods with unreferenced-status prose.
3. **What are the literal original private names?** Symbols are stripped. Names in this report are selected from command consumers, payload shape, established project style and caller semantics. Resolved for first draft: use the human names here; do not defer or preserve `sub_...`.
4. **Was the folder literally `network`?** Exact historical folder spelling is not recoverable, but current project topology and accepted Socket root use `NexusTK/network`. Resolved for this project: preserve that path.
5. **Should feature packet builders move into one sender file?** Their code, data, classes, dialogs and opcodes are co-located with feature implementations, and only the Socket call is shared. Resolved: keep feature-local.
6. **Can the friend-list body use source-closed container/string declarations without inventing helpers?** Yes. Current `StringBase.h` declares `mystr::StringBase`'s converting constructor, `c_str`, `length`, `empty`, and `mystr::WideRangeCompare`. Resolved: use two `std::set<mystr::StringBase<wchar_t>, WideFriendNameLess>` instances over the exact Config array and a converting `mystr::StringBase<char>` for the exact 12-byte record copy. No helper declaration remains pending; emitted order still comes from the second original-slot pass, never counting-set iteration.
7. **Are the remaining Socket field names exact originals?** The binary fixes every offset, capacity, ordering, use family, and total size, but stripped symbols do not prove six private spellings. Resolved: use the human names in the formal H (`m_serialWriteActive`, `m_serialState`, `m_socketReadPending`, `m_frameState`, `m_packetBuffer`, `m_encodedPacketBuffer`) under the exact `0x3aa54` layout assertion. This evidence-bounded naming inference is a confidence cap, not deferred research or a source-closure blocker.
8. **Is `BuildHandshakeBlock` source-closed despite IDA rendering an `int __stdcall(size_t Size, void *Block)` prestate?** UID0003SP and the accepted definition establish that the human-source API returns `void`; callers do not consume a return value, and the parameters are the seed length and seed block. Resolved and applied disposition: formal H is `void BuildHandshakeBlock(size_t seedLength, void *seedBlock);`; dated command `000000025750` and its callback snapshot confirmed the exact ABI, while Gate 2A must dynamically reread current ordinary and generated state.
9. **Will the public MCP parser accept the A01-A18 type contracts?** The first A01 anonymous-parameter request returned exact error `Not a function type: void __thiscall(Socket *, char, void *)`; the second A01 request with named parameters but no function name returned exact error `Not a function type: void __thiscall(Socket *this, char arg_0, void *arg_4)`. Installed `_parse_function_tinfo` uses `parse_decl(..., PT_SIL | PT_TYP)`, and its fixture accepts a complete declaration containing a function name. Resolved at report-contract level: every literal `set_type` request now contains the exact destination function name, receiver `this`, and exact post-frame parameter identifiers (`arg_0`, `arg_4`, `Src`, or `packetSize` as applicable). Both failed workers were discarded without save; fresh Gate 2B must still verify parser acceptance and the complete deterministic readback before persistence.
10. **Are P06's `unk_...` spellings display-only, and can the report protect P06c's entire item model?** The older transaction snapshots returned empty public names, but the current canonical-hash read-only session returns literal public names `unk_69BA40/41/42/43/46/47`, `unk_69BA5C`, `unk_69BA60`, `unk_69BA64`, and `unk_69BA94`; they are therefore exact current no-change prestates. Current P06c exposes a four-byte typed head followed by independently undefined one-byte heads, and the report did not serialize the full partition. Resolved: preserve all `0x400` bytes and both xrefs, verify only four explicitly serialized item samples, and make no whole-range physical-item-model promise.
11. **Must persisted function types, physical frames, decompiler output, aggregate profiles, and direct public graph reads agree?** The supervisor's isolated unsaved TX proved they are distinct authorities. Resolved: A08's persisted const pointee coexists with physical `Src:void *`; A17's no-argument persisted type coexists with undeletable physical `arg_4`; A15's raw frame deterministically shifts `+4`; A06/A15 remain valid retained-raw functions despite exact bounded decompile failures; public `callees` overrides A08's spurious aggregate self edge; `xref_query.total` overrides modeled-only `func_profile.caller_count`; and `func_profile` is retained only for exact bounds/size. Failed stack probes are diagnostics and add no mutation rows.

No unresolved research item blocks the target score, ownership decision, or accepted source output. The supervisor's unsaved VerifyOnly enumeration proves parser acceptance and the reconciled live in-memory A/P poststate for that isolated TX, including the expected A06/A15 decompile failures and A08/A15/A17 frame results. It does not prove persistence: no `idb_save` occurred, physical TX SHA256 remained at its preflight hash, and saved-TX, `DB_VERIFY`, promotion, `DB_FINAL`, failed-state classification, restore, and final physical/semantic closure remain pending under the complete Section 21 contract.
